<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                                                                                                                                                                <picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/tikhonova-av-round.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/tikhonova-av-round.png" alt="Anastasia Tikhonova" class="author__image webpexpress-processed"></picture>

                                                        <div class="author__name title-h6">Anastasia Tikhonova</div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">The Art of Cyberwarfare</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Chinese APTs attack Russia</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">August 3, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Advanced Persistent Threats</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-36.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-36.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">APT</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">China</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Malware</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In mid-May 2021, experts from SOLAR JSOC and the National Computer Incident Response &amp; Coordination Center (NCIRCC) released a joint report on a series of targeted attacks detected in 2020. According to the <a href="https://rt-solar.ru/analytics/reports/2203/" rel="nofollow">report</a>, the attackers targeted Russian federal executive authorities.</p>
<p>While analyzing the report, <strong>Anastasia Tikhonova</strong> (Head of APT Research at Group-IB) and <strong>Dmitry Kupin</strong> (Senior Malware Analyst) noticed that they had already come across similar tools in earlier attacks.</p>
<p><strong>Chinese APTs are one of the most numerous and aggressive hacker communities.</strong> Several dozen groups conduct attacks in countries all over the world, and Russia is no exception. Hackers mostly target state agencies, industrial facilities, military contractors, and research institutes. The main objective is espionage: attackers gain access to confidential data and attempt to hide their presence for as long as possible. There have been cases when attackers successfully persisted in the victim&#8217;s network for several years.</p>
<p>Unfortunately, the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report did not provide indicators of compromise, so the experts had to rely on descriptions of the functionality and screenshots of the malicious code. As a result, Group-IB&#8217;s researchers came up with some interesting conclusions about which <strong>Chinese groups could be behind the attacks against Russian federal executive authorities in 2020</strong>, what tools they used, and how their malware has evolved since.</p>
<h2>Key conclusions</h2>
<ul>
<li>The research describes Webdav-O malware detected in attacks against Russian federal executive authorities in 2020.</li>
<li>Group-IB experts detected two versions of the Webdav-O Trojan for x86 and x64 systems.</li>
<li>When comparing parts of the code, the specialists proved that the Webdav-O x64 Trojan was used in attacks against Russian federal executive authorities. The malware has existed since at least 2018.</li>
<li>Group-IB specialists established that Webdav-O has a set of commands similar to a popular Trojan called BlueTraveller (aka RemShell), which was developed in China and has been linked to the hacker group called TaskMasters.</li>
<li>Before that, Sentinel Labs released a report about malware called Mail-O, which was also identified in attacks against Russian federal executive authorities. Mail-O was deemed to be linked to the Chinese hacker group TA428.</li>
<li>Group TA428 is known to use a Trojan called Albaniiutas in their attacks. Group-IB&#8217;s analysis showed that Albaniiutas is an updated version of BlueTraveller.</li>
<li>Group-IB experts believe that either both Chinese hacker groups (TA428 and TaskMasters) attacked Russian federal executive authorities in 2020 or that there is one united Chinese hacker group made up of different units.</li>
</ul>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5"><strong>TA428</strong> <strong>is a Chinese state-sponsored hacker group</strong> that has been operating since 2013. The attackers target a number of government agencies in East Asia that control governmental information technology, domestic and foreign policy, and economic development.

<strong>TaskMasters (aka BlueTraveller) is a state-sponsored Chinese hacker group</strong> that allegedly has been active since at least 2010. The group attacks companies based in several countries, but many of their targets are located in Russia and CIS. The hackers target solid industrial and energy enterprises, government agencies, and transport companies.</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                                                                                    <div class="author__name"><span class="light-blue-gradient"></span></div>
                                                                                                                <div class="author__description body2"></div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Starting point</h2>
<ul>
<li>As the experts put it: &#8220;<em>The report dwells on the analysis of a series of targeted attacks</em>&#8220;. Based on this information, we assumed that several hacker groups may be behind the attacks.</li>
<li>The attackers used malware that interacted with management server via the cloud service called Yandex.Disk. The malware was dubbed <strong>Webdav-O</strong>.</li>
<li>Attackers also used malicious software that accessed the cloud service Mail.ru. The malware was dubbed <strong>Mail-O</strong>.</li>
</ul>
<p>In early June 2021, analysts from the American cybersecurity company Sentinel Labs released a <a href="https://www.sentinelone.com/labs/thundercats-hack-the-fsb-your-taxes-didnt-pay-for-this-op/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">report</a> about Mail-O. The experts wrote that Mail-O is a version of the relatively well-known malware called SManager, which is used by the Chinese hacker group TA428.</p>
<p>Group-IB specialists wanted to make sure that Mail-O is loader, while Smanager and Tmanger are Remote Access Trojans (RAT). However, a part of the code overlaps in the exported functions &#8220;Entery&#8221; and &#8220;ServiceMain&#8221; of Mail-O, SManager and Tmanger, which brings us back to TA428. Moreover, hackers from TA428 have already been found to be involved in espionage against Russia, especially Russian state facilities.</p>
<p>To prove the hypothesis that TA428 was behind the attacks against Russian federal executive authorities in 2020, we decided to analyze a sample of Webdav-O. Group-IB Threat Intelligence &amp; Attribution has detected similar malicious behavior before and can now explain why we link it to a specific group. Below we provide an analysis of Webdav-O samples and highlight features that overlap with the points mentioned in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report.</p>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5"><strong>骑驴找马 [qí lǘ zhǎo mǎ]</strong>

<strong>Verbatim translation</strong>: Ride a mule while looking for a horse.

<strong>Definition</strong>: Use the tools you have while looking for something better.</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                                                                                    <div class="author__name"><span class="light-blue-gradient"></span></div>
                                                                                                                <div class="author__description body2"></div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Analysis of Webdav-O sample</h2>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Name</td>
<td width="75%">1.dll</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">SHA1</td>
<td width="75%">c9e03855f738e360d24018e2d203142c7ae6c2ec</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Compilation timestamp</td>
<td width="75%">2018-07-12 03:08:01</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">First Submission</td>
<td width="75%">2019-11-07 10:34:11</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Dll Name</td>
<td width="75%">y_dll.dll</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Export function</td>
<td width="75%">ServiceMain</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>File &#8220;<em>1.dll</em>&#8221; is an x86 dynamic link library (DLL) that functions as a service in the system.</p>
<p>The analyzed file provides remote access to the command line shell (cmd.exe) and executes various commands originating from C2 on the compromised host.</p>
<p>The legitimate cloud service called Yandex.Disk (webdav.yandex.ru:443) is used as network infrastructure, namely C&amp;C. Network interaction with the cloud is implemented via the Webdav protocol. The authentication method is Basic.</p>
<p>The strings and configuration data are encrypted with the RC4 algorithm using the following key: { <strong>8A 4F 01 47 34 C9 75 F8 2B C8 C1 E9 D2 F3 A5 8B</strong> }. The key size is 16 bytes. The analyzed files can work with 1-7 accounts (in this case only 2 are used, but we will come back to this later).</p>
<h2>Features of the sample</h2>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25716 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%201304'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="functionality of the webdav-0 x86 sample" width="1500" height="1304" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4.jpg"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25716 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4.jpg" alt="functionality of the webdav-0 x86 sample" width="1500" height="1304"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>1. The exported ServiceMain function uses a random delay before the main code is executed.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25717 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20265%20124'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The exported ServiceMain function code" width="265" height="124" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-17.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25717 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-17.png" alt="The exported ServiceMain function code" width="265" height="124"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>2. Yandex.Disk cloud accounts are checked for availability using the query &#8220;/?userinfo&#8221; (GET).</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25718 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20468%20279'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Yandex.Disk cloud part of the code" width="468" height="279" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-17.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25718 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-17.png" alt="Yandex.Disk cloud part of the code" width="468" height="279"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>3. The file &#8220;<em>/test3.txt</em>&#8221; is uploaded from &#8220;Yandex.Disk&#8221; (GET) and checked for the &#8220;<em>Just A Test!</em>&#8221; line. In case of success, the system checks for batch files in the &#8220;<em>/test&#8221; directory of &#8220;Yandex.Disk</em>&#8221; (PROFIND).</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25719 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20468%20310'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="file /test3.txt uploaded from Yandex.Disk" width="468" height="310" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-17.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25719 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-17.png" alt="file /test3.txt uploaded from Yandex.Disk" width="468" height="310"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>4. A command file is defined for downloading from the Yandex.Disk cloud (GET). The response from the server is processed. The name of the file with commands is between the tags:</p>
<p><em>&lt;d:href&gt;[name of the command file]&lt;/d:href&gt;</em></p>
</div>
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>5. In the command file, the contents are encrypted using the RC4 algorithm. After downloading the command file, it is deleted from Yandex.Disk (DELETE).</p>
<p>6. The file &#8220;<em>/test2.txt</em>&#8221; is uploaded to Yandex.Disk (PUT). The file &#8220;<em>/test2.txt</em>&#8221; contains the line &#8220;<em>Just A Test!</em>&#8220;. The mechanism is presumably used to check the functioning of a malicious program.</p>
<p>7. The file &#8220;<em>/test2/[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin</em>&#8221; is uploaded to &#8220;Yandex.Disk&#8221; (PUT). The file contains the command results. Data is encrypted using the RC4 algorithm.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25720 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20468%20282'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="file /test2/[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin" width="468" height="282" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-17.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25720 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-17.png" alt="file /test2/[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin" width="468" height="282"></noscript></picture></p>
<h2>Description of the commands</h2>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Command</th>
<th width="75%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">-upload</td>
<td width="75%">Uploads the file to Yandex.Disk cloud storage. The file name is specified in the command. The file is saved in the cloud under the following name: &#8220;[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin&#8221;. Response format: &#8220;##u## %s %s&#8221;.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">-download</td>
<td width="75%">Downloads the file from Yandex.Disk cloud storage. The file name is specified in the command. The downloaded file is deleted from Yandex.Disk. Response format: &#8220;##d## %s&#8221;.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">-quit</td>
<td width="75%">Ends a session (exits the command execution flow).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">-setsleep</td>
<td width="75%">Sets the waiting interval (in minutes) between command requests. Response format: &#8220;##s## %d&#8221;.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">[other command]</td>
<td width="75%">Runs the command in the command line shell (cmd.exe).</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Comparison with the sample presented in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report</h2>
<p>When analyzing the code uploaded to VirusTotal, we found many overlapping points with the Trojan described in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report. Some of the common features can be seen in the screenshot with the malware code, which shows the receipt of the command files list in the test folder:</p>
<div id="attachment_25721" style="width: 962px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-14.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25721" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25721 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20952%20312'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Comparison of the Webdav-O sample from the report to the VirusTotal sample" width="952" height="312" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-14.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25721" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25721 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-14.png" alt="Comparison of the Webdav-O sample from the report to the VirusTotal sample" width="952" height="312"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25721" class="wp-caption-text">Comparison of the Webdav-O sample from the report (on the left) to the VirusTotal sample (on the right)</p></div>
</div>
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Comparison of Webdav-O samples</h2>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="50%;50%">
<tbody>

<p>As you can see from our comparison of the two samples, Webdav-O from the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report looks like a newer, partially improved version of the Trojan that we detected on VirusTotal.</p>
<h2>Comparison of Webdav-O with the code of the BlueTraveller (RemShell) sample</h2>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5"><strong>见风转舵 [jiàn fēng zhuǎn duò]</strong>

<strong>Verbatim translation</strong>: If you feel the wind – change direction.

<strong>Meaning</strong>: Change your tactics to avoid difficulties.</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                                                                                    <div class="author__name"><span class="light-blue-gradient"></span></div>
                                                                                                                <div class="author__description body2"></div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Based on a large database of analyzed malicious samples accumulated when searching and responding to cyber threats, Group-IB&#8217;s specialists linked the detected Webdav-O sample to the <strong>BlueTraveller Trojan</strong>.</p>
<p>To prove our hypothesis, below we present a comparison of the Webdav-O x86 sample and the sample of BlueTraveller (RemShell) (SHA1: 6857BB2C3AE5F9C2393D9F88816BE7A10CB5573F).</p>

<ol>
<li>Similar DLL name (DIRECTORY_ENTRY_EXPORT &#8211; original DLL name)</li>
<li>Same command names</li>
<li>Same principle of command processing</li>
<li>Feature allowing to execute commands in the command line shell (cmd.exe)</li>
</ol>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-8.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25687 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20436%20253'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" width="436" height="253" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-8.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25687 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-8.png" alt="" width="436" height="253"></noscript></picture></p>
<h2>Accounts, passwords, and attribution</h2>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5"><strong>路遥知马力, 日久见人心 [lù yáo zhī mǎ lì rì jiǔ jiàn rén xīn]</strong>

<strong>Verbatim translation</strong>: Having overcome a long distance, you will know a horse's endurance, and after a long time you will know what lies in a person's heart.

<strong>Definition</strong>: Time reveals a person's true nature.</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                                                                                    <div class="author__name"><span class="light-blue-gradient"></span></div>
                                                                                                                <div class="author__description body2"></div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Let&#8217;s go back to the analyzed sample of <strong>Webdav-O x86</strong>. When we decrypted the malware string, we found the following &#8220;login:password&#8221; for the attacker&#8217;s accounts used on Yandex.Disk.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-7.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25688 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20512%20248'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="sample of Webdav-O x86 code" width="512" height="248" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-7.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25688 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-7.png" alt="sample of Webdav-O x86 code" width="512" height="248"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>The data discovered:</p>
<ul>
<li>aleshaadams:7ujm!QAZ2wsx</li>
<li>tstrobos:&amp;UJM1qaz2ws</li>
</ul>
<p>If the account login is known, it is possible to recover the email address as follows:</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="9de9eee9eff2fff2eedde4fcf3f9f8e5b3efe8">[email&#160;protected]</a></li>
<li><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="3958555c4a5158585d58544a794058575d5c41174b4c">[email&#160;protected]</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-617"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Attempt to recover the password in yandex" title="17-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min.jpg" alt="Attempt to recover the password in yandex" title="17-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Attempt to recover the password for <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="21404d445249404045404c526158404f4544590f5354">[email&#160;protected]</a></p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Attempt to recover the password in yandex" title="18-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min.jpg" alt="Attempt to recover the password in yandex" title="18-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Attempt to recover the password for <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="89fdfafdfbe6ebe6fac9f0e8e7edecf1a7fbfc">[email&#160;protected]</a></p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The screenshots show that both accounts are linked to cellphone numbers in the same region (+86), which is the country code for China.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-6.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25691 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20512%20131'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="cellphone number country code" width="512" height="131" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-6.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25691 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-6.png" alt="cellphone number country code" width="512" height="131"></noscript></picture></p>
<h2>Analysis of password generation</h2>
<p>In 2019, Elmar Nabigaev (Deputy Director of Expert Security Center Positive Technologies) <a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XYuclHsoQO4&amp;t=698s" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">delivered</a> a report entitled &#8220;<strong>The TaskMasters APT</strong>&#8221; (aka BlueTraveller) and gave examples of passwords discovered when investigating the malware campaign:</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25692 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201489%201064'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="comparison of taskmasters password creation templates" width="1489" height="1064" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min.jpg"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25692 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min.jpg" alt="comparison of taskmasters password creation templates" width="1489" height="1064"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>The images above show that the passwords to the Webdav-O account were generated using a similar technique as TaskMasters. The only things that changed were the registry and the key row combination.</p>
<h2>Blurring the boundaries</h2>
<p>Considering all the comparisons made and the information discovered about the accounts, we believe that the Chinese hacker group <strong>TaskMasters</strong> is most likely behind the attacks involving an improved version of the Webdav-O Trojan. The case of TA428, however, is still open to debate. Could both of them be behind the attack against Russian federal executive authorities in 2020? Could there be someone else involved? Or was it the same group?</p>
<p>We will continue our investigation and seek more information for analysis. Let us take a look at the report about TA428 and their new tools, in particular the Trojan called Albaniiutas, which was released by NTT Security Corporation in 2020.</p>
<div id="attachment_25693" style="width: 582px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25693" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25693 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20572%20350'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Executing Albaniiutas files, NTT report" width="572" height="350" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25693" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25693 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-5.png" alt="Executing Albaniiutas files, NTT report" width="572" height="350"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25693" class="wp-caption-text">Executing Albaniiutas files, NTT report</p></div>
</div>
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The aim of our investigation is to study these two objects. Our reasoning will be presented below.</p>
<p>First and foremost, we discovered some common points in the utility used to launch DLL:</p>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The parts of code above show that the code in BlueTraveller is less sophisticated, but in both cases the separator &#8220;\b&#8221; is used three times (the strtok function). Below is an example of the data that Albaniiutas malware receives for each command:</p>
<div id="attachment_25704" style="width: 657px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25704" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25704 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20647%20165'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Format of the data received when executing commands" width="647" height="165" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25704" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25704 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-3.png" alt="Format of the data received when executing commands" width="647" height="165"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25704" class="wp-caption-text">Format of the data received when executing commands (retrieved from the NTT report)</p></div>
<ol>
<li>If the command is executed multiple times, the command will not be executed unless a value other than the previous one is specified.</li>
<li>Separator</li>
<li>If the value does not match the value in ③, the command will not be executed.</li>
<li>Command identifier and command parameters separated by spaces.</li>
</ol>
<p>Let&#8217;s also compare the code fragments for checking and executing the commands received from the C&amp;C server:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-328"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Fragments of code in BlueTraveller" title="33-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-3.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-3.png" alt="Fragments of code in BlueTraveller" title="33-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Fragments of code in BlueTraveller</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Fragments of code in Albaniiutas" title="34-min" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min.webp"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min.webp" alt="Fragments of code in Albaniiutas" title="34-min"></noscript><p class="small wp-caption-text">Fragments of code in Albaniiutas</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>
                 
                  <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                    var carousel328 = new Swiper('.gallery-swiper-328', {
                      grabCursor: true,
                      autoHeight: true,
                      effect: "fade",
                      observer: true,
                      observeParents:true,

                        crossFade: true,

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>It is clear that this part was updated by the hackers, but the commands remain the same:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="24%;36%;36%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="24%">Command</th>
<th width="36%">Options</th>
<th width="36%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="24%">-exit</td>
<td width="36%"></td>
<td width="36%">Terminates the function for receiving and processing commands (exiting the flow)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="24%">-download</td>
<td width="36%">Downloads URLs or Path to the storage directory</td>
<td width="36%">Downloads a file from the C&amp;C server</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="24%">-upload</td>
<td width="36%">Path to the file on the infected device or Part of path of the URL-address during the upload</td>
<td width="36%">Uploads a file to the C&amp;C server</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="24%">(command)</td>
<td width="36%">Command arguments</td>
<td width="36%">Executes the command with cmd.exe and returns the result to the C&amp;C server.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In addition, the two Trojans have a similar pattern of communicating with the control server in the protocols of network interaction with the C&amp;C server. Below is an example of network communication with the C&amp;C server, taken from BlueTraveller samples available on VirusTotal.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="60%;40%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="60%">BlueTraveller</th>
<th width="40%">Albaniiutas</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="60%">http://45.32.188[.]226/0000/1301/0024/4u/i7fr09bGus+Wyt7iyjos=</td>
<td width="40%">http://go.vegispaceshop[.]org/home/2252/0108/IKNYF6oSkYtpU60GfnS27fbRKdcvCj/kZgeNi/ 8QIRN2+6+O3gKV6ODd2mEPNo7tYi1+ePOSmFzMlUY1ciJ1 A0XSw8aOkJzTDXITxdSNo74=</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="60%">Template: [IP]/[0000 or 1111]/[0-9]{4}/[0-9]{4}/[base64 data]</td>
<td width="40%">Template: [domain]/[dir]/[0-9]{4}/[0-9]{4}/[base64 data]</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Let&#8217;s move on to string obfuscation in Albaniiutas. We have established that strings are encrypted using the RC4 algorithm. The encryption key used is <strong>L!Q@W#E$R%T^Y&amp;U*A|}t~k</strong>.</p>
<p>The same encryption key was used in the BlueTraveller server component which stores the log files in the encrypted form:</p>
<div id="attachment_25707" style="width: 522px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25707" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25707 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20512%20230'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="A fragment of code with a line written to a log file" width="512" height="230" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25707" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25707 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-2.png" alt="A fragment of code with a line written to a log file" width="512" height="230"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25707" class="wp-caption-text">A fragment of code with a line written to a log file (retrieved from <a href="https://www.ptsecurity.com/ru-ru/research/analytics/operation-taskmasters-2019/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">PTSecurity &#8220;Operation TaskMasters&#8221;</a> 2019)</p></div>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25708 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20512%20429'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" width="512" height="429" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-3.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25708 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-3.png" alt="" width="512" height="429"></noscript></picture></p>
<p><strong>The conclusion is clear: Albaniiutas is nothing but a logic continuation of the malware belonging to the BlueTraveller family.</strong></p>
<h2>And then it dawned on us…</h2>
<p>We thought that we had analyzed everything and that we were done with comparisons, when suddenly a sample was uploaded to VirusTotal. We identified it as Webdav-O.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Name</td>
<td width="75%">y_dll.dll</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">SHA1</td>
<td width="75%">3ff73686244ca128103e86d8c5aa024e37e7b86d</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Compilation timestamp</td>
<td width="75%">2018-12-06 11:15:35</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">First Submission</td>
<td width="75%">2021-06-05 04:41:00</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Dll Name</td>
<td width="75%">y_dll.dll</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">Export function</td>
<td width="75%">ServiceMain</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The file &#8220;y_dll.dll&#8221; is an x64 dynamic link library (DLL) that functions as a service in the system.</p>
<p>As can be seen, this version of Webdav-O was written for a system with a different bitness and compiled later than our sample of Webdav-O x86 (2018-12 and 2018-07, respectively).</p>
<p>The legitimate cloud service Yandex.Disk (webdav.yandex.ru:443) is also used as a network infrastructure, in particular C2. Network interaction with the cloud is carried out via a Webdav protocol.</p>
<p>However, this sample supports two authentication methods instead of one in Webdav-O x86: Basic (with a username and password) and OAuth (using a token).</p>
<p>The strings and configuration data are encrypted using the RC4 algorithm with the following key: { <strong>C3 02 03 04 05 DD EE 08 09 10 11 12 1F D2 15 16</strong> }. The key size is 16 bytes. The analyzed file can work with 1-7 accounts (it works with only one in this case).</p>
<p>This sample seemed even more similar to the one described in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report: unlike our sample, it has the &#8220;-sleepuntil&#8221; function.</p>
<p>Unfortunately colleagues at SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC did not provide any indicators of compromise, so we can only make comparisons based on screenshots and descriptions of the capabilities of their sample.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-739"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Webdav-O sample from the report" title="37-min" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min.webp"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min.webp" alt="Webdav-O sample from the report" title="37-min"></noscript><p class="small wp-caption-text">Webdav-O sample from the report</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Webdav-O x64 sample" title="38-min" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/38-min.webp"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/38-min.webp" alt="Webdav-O x64 sample" title="38-min"></noscript><p class="small wp-caption-text">Webdav-O x64 sample</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>
                 
                  <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">

                  <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The parts of code presented above show that both versions look identical. Group-IB experts also noticed that in Webdav-O x64, the commands and their results are transferred by uploading various files to Yandex.Disk:</p>
<p>Description of files created by <strong>Webdav-O from the report</strong>:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>test2.txt, test3.txt.</strong> are files used to check the connection</li>
<li><strong>test4.txt</strong> contains information about the interval (minutes) between command requests to the server</li>
<li><strong>test5.txt</strong> contains the launch date for the malware</li>
<li><strong>test7.txt</strong> is uploaded to the server and contains a 16-byte RC4 key that is used to encrypt commands and their results (the</li>
<li><strong>key</strong> is also encrypted with a public RSA key)</li>
<li><strong>test</strong> is a directory containing files that are downloaded, decrypted, and processed as commands. Malware receives the file list via the PROPFIND request and by parsing the necessary tags: &lt;d:href&gt;complete path to file&lt;/d:href&gt;.</li>
</ul>
<p>Description of the files created by Webdav-O x64:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="20%;70%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="20%">File/ Directory</th>
<th width="70%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="20%">test2.txt, test3.txt</td>
<td width="70%">Used to verify the connection. Example of &#8220;test2.txt&#8221; content: &#8220;Just A Test!&#8221;</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">test4.txt</td>
<td width="70%">Contains the waiting interval (in minutes) between command requests. Example of &#8220;test4.txt&#8221; content: 15</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">test5.txt</td>
<td width="70%">Contains the date and time until which the malware will be in sleep mode. Format: %d-%d-%d_%d:%d:%d, example of file &#8220;test5.txt&#8221; content: 2021-03-02_14:30:00</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">test6.txt</td>
<td width="70%">Contains an OAuth token. The content is encrypted using the RC4 algorithm with the following key: { 8A 4F 01 47 34 C9 75 F8 2B C8 C1 E9 D2 F3 A5 8B } (16 bytes). It is noteworthy that this key has already been used by another sample of our Webdav-O x86 to encrypt strings and configuration data.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">test7.txt</td>
<td width="70%">It is loaded onto the server and contains a RC4 session key (16 bytes), which is used to encrypt commands and their results (the key itself is encrypted with a public RSA key). RC4 session keys are generated using the BCryptGenRandom function: BCryptGenRandom(0i64, rc4_key_session, 16u, BCRYPT_USE_SYSTEM_PREFERRED_RNG)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">/test</td>
<td width="70%">Contains files that are downloaded, decrypted, and processed as commands. File name format: &#8220;/test/[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin&#8221;</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">/test2</td>
<td width="70%">Contains files (results of executed commands), which are encrypted and uploaded to Yandex.Disk. File name format: &#8220;/test2/[0-9]{1,4}[0-9]{1,4}.bin&#8221;</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The data presented above shows that this part is also identical except for the description of test6.txt, which is not presented in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report.</p>
<p>Based on the comparisons above, Group-IB experts have concluded that this particular Webdav-O sample was most likely used in attacks on Russian federal executive authorities in 2020 and it is the same Trojan as the one described in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRCC report.</p>
<h2>To sum up&#8230;</h2>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5"><strong>人心齐，泰山移 [rén xīn qí, tài shān yí]</strong>

<strong>Verbatim translation</strong>: United, people can move even Mount Taishan.

<strong>Definition</strong>: By working together people can accomplish anything.</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                                                                                    <div class="author__name"><span class="light-blue-gradient"></span></div>
                                                                                                                <div class="author__description body2"></div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><div id="attachment_25713" style="width: 1510px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/41-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25713" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25713 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%201373'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Venn diagram showing the common points between the two Trojans" width="1500" height="1373" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/41-min.jpg"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25713" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-25713 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/41-min.jpg" alt="Venn diagram showing the common points between the two Trojans" width="1500" height="1373"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25713" class="wp-caption-text">Venn diagram showing the common points between the two Trojans (Only data presented in the blog is used in the diagram)</p></div>
<ul>
<li>Webdav-O malware is a version of the BlueTraveller (RemShell) Trojan, which is classified as a Chinese APT. Webdav-O was designed for both x86 and x64 systems.</li>
<li>Webdav-O may have been used by the Chinese APT TaskMasters (aka BlueTraveller). Based on the information about attacks on various federal executive authorities in 2020, presented in the SOLAR JSOC and NCIRC report, it is possible that in some cases the Chinese APT TA428 was behind the attacks, while others could have been performed by TaskMasters.</li>
<li>Researchers from SentinelLabs have linked Mail-O to Smanager and Tmanger (tools used by TA428). Group-IB specialists found common code parts in the malware&#8217;s exported functions &#8220;Entery&#8221; and &#8220;ServiceMain&#8221;. We can say with moderate confidence that Mail-O was developed by TA428.</li>
<li>Based on research done by NTT Security, it can be said that TA428 has already used the malware Albaniiutas. Group-IB experts have shown that the Trojan is a new version of BlueTraveller (RemShell). As such, it can be assumed that Webdav-O is also linked to TA428.</li>
<li>It is noteworthy that Chinese hacker groups actively exchange tools and infrastructure, but perhaps it is just the case here.</li>
<li>There is also strong evidence that points to one large hacker group consisting of several intelligence units of the People&#8217;s Liberation Army of China. For example, unit 61398 from Shanghai is responsible for the actions of a well-known group called <a href="https://www.fireeye.com/blog/threat-research/2013/02/mandiant-exposes-apt1-chinas-cyber-espionage-units.html" rel="nofollow">APT1</a> (aka Comment Crew), and unit 61419 from Qingdao has been linked to <a href="https://www.japantimes.co.jp/news/2021/04/20/national/chinese-military-japan-cyberattacks/" rel="nofollow">Tick</a>. Each unit attacks to the fullest, according to a strict timeline and order. This means that one Trojan can be configured and modified by hackers from different departments with different levels of training and with various objectives.</li>
</ul>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/42-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25714 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20698%20684'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" width="698" height="684" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/42-min-3.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25714 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/42-min-3.png" alt="" width="698" height="684"></noscript></picture></p>

                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion911 = document.querySelector('.accordionList911');
                          if (accordion911) {
                            let items = accordion911.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion911.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {
                              let thisItem = this.parentNode;
                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });
                      </script>

                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="promo-block"><h2 class="title-h3 mb-xs-16 mb-sm-16 mb-24">Try Group-IB Threat Intelligence now</h2><p>Defeat threats efficiently and identify attackers proactively with a revolutionary cyber threat intelligence platform by Group-IB</p><div class="buttons-wrapper mt-md-48 mt-sm-32 mt-xs-24"><a href="https://www.group-ib.com/products/threat-intelligence/" class="button button--filled button--md "><span class="effect slide-up"><span class="effect__label">Request a demo</span><span class="effect__label" aria-hidden="true">Request a demo</span></span></a></div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>YARA rule</h2>

import "pe"

rule webdavo_rat
{
  meta:
    author = "Dmitry Kupin"
    company = "Group-IB"
    family = "webdavo.rat"
    description = "Suspected Webdav-O RAT (YaDisk)"
    sample = "7874c9ab2828bc3bf920e8cdee027e745ff059237c61b7276bbba5311147ebb6" // x86
    sample = "849e6ed87188de6dc9f2ef37e7c446806057677c6e05a367abbd649784abdf77" // x64
    severity = 9
    date = "2021-06-10"

  strings:
    $rc4_key_0 = { 8A 4F 01 47 34 C9 75 F8 2B C8 C1 E9 D2 F3 A5 8B }
    $rc4_key_1 = { C3 02 03 04 05 DD EE 08 09 10 11 12 1F D2 15 16 }
    $s0 = "y_dll.dll" fullword ascii
    $s1 = "test3.txt" fullword ascii
    $s2 = "DELETE" fullword wide
    $s3 = "PROPFIND" fullword wide

  condition:
    (any of ($rc4_key*) or 3 of ($s*)) or
    (
     pe.imphash() == "43021febc8494d66a8bc60d0fa953473" or
     pe.imphash() == "68320a454321f215a3b6fcd7d585626b"
    )
}

rule albaniiutas_dropper_exe
{
  meta:
    author = "Dmitry Kupin"
    company = "Group-IB"

    sample = "71750c58eee35107db1a8e4d583f3b1a918dbffbd42a6c870b100a98fd0342e0" // csrss.exe dropped from 690bf6b83...
    sample = "83b619f65d49afbb76c849c3f5315dbcb4d2c7f4ddf89ac93c26977e85105f32" // dropper_stage_0 with decoy
    sample = "690bf6b83cecbf0ac5c5f4939a9283f194b1a8815a62531a000f3020fee2ec42" // dropper_stage_0 with decoy
    severity = 9
    date = "2021-07-06"

  strings:
    $eventname = /[0-9A-F]{8}-[0-9A-F]{4}-4551-8F84-08E738AEC[0-9A-F]{3}/ fullword ascii wide
    $rc4_key = { 00 4C 21 51 40 57 23 45 24 52 25 54 5E 59 26 55 2A 41 7C 7D 74 7E 6B 00 } // L!Q@W#E$R%T^Y&U*A|}t~k

    $s1 = "FileVJCr error" fullword ascii

    $s3 = "zlib err0r" fullword ascii
    $s4 = "De err0r" fullword ascii
    $s5 = "CreateFileW_CH error!" fullword ascii
    $s6 = "GetConfigOffset error!" fullword ascii

  condition:
    5 of them or
    (
     pe.imphash() == "222e118fa8c0eafeef102e49953507b9" or

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
